**FR-32 Theme choice (M35).** Until M35 the app was dark only. Asked by the owner (2026-11-10); the owner chose the light colours, the place in Settings, the default and the launch behaviour from mockups the same day.
- **Choices:** Light, Dark and System. **System is the default**, for new installs and for people updating alike, so after the update a phone in light mode shows the app light. System follows the phone's dark mode and changes with it while the app is open.
- **Where:** a new Settings category, **Appearance**, the first row of the list (FR-31), icon `Icons.contrast`, subtitle "Theme: Light", "Theme: Dark" or "Theme: System". It opens `/settings/appearance` titled "Appearance": a "Theme" section label, then three radio rows, Light, Dark, and System with the subtitle "Follows the phone's dark mode". Tapping a row applies it at once, with no Save button and no restart.
- **Saved:** in shared preferences under `appearance.theme` (`light`, `dark` or `system`), read before the first Flutter frame, so the app never draws a frame in the wrong theme. Nothing saved, or a value the app doesn't know, means System.
- **Light colours** (owner's choice C: one deeper green on a soft mint page; the bright green fails as text on white, 1.9:1):

  | Role | Dark (unchanged) | Light |
  |---|---|---|
  | Page background | `#0E1311` | `#F5F8F6` |
  | Cards and panels (surface) | `#18201C` | `#FFFFFF` |
  | Accent: buttons, switches, tabs, links, selection | `#3DD68C` | `#0F7D4C` (5.2:1 on white) |
  | Text and icons on the accent | `#06140D` | `#FFFFFF` |
  | Text | `#EEF3F0` | `#0E1311` |
  | Muted text, hints, subtitles | `#A3AFA8` | `#5B6862` (5.5:1 on the page) |

  The two themes differ in colour only: fonts, sizes, the list-row hierarchy and the text cap (§4) are the same.
- **Everything goes light** (owner's decision): every screen and sheet, the video areas included (the players' backgrounds and letterboxing, the crop editor's panels, the scene strip, the person chooser, the waveform, Done's player bar).
- **What never follows the theme:**
  - **The exported reel.** Caption text, its backing, the branding pill and logo in the overlay PNG (`overlay_painter.dart`) and the same parts of the reel preview (`reel_preview.dart`) keep their colours, so a reel looks the same whichever theme made it.
  - **The logo:** the green tile `#3DD68C` with ink `#06140D` (§7), in both themes, as on the launcher icon.
  - **Marks drawn on the video picture** (Claude's call): the dimming outside the crop window, the crop frame and its handles, the face and body corners and their number tags, and the dark scrims under player controls keep their dark-theme colours, because the picture, not the page, is behind them.
- **Launch (FR-20, owner's choice B):** the native launch screen follows the phone's dark mode, not the saved choice, because it shows before the app can read it. Flutter's launch animation matches the native screen (the phone's mode), then its exit fade reveals Home in the chosen theme; when the two differ, that fade is the only change.

**FR-33 Fade at the ends (M38).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-11): a clip trimmed elsewhere starts and stops on a hard cut.
- **Switch:** "Fade in and out" on Fine-tune · Video's Trim tab, under the nudge cards, with the hint "The picture fades in from black and out to black. The sound doesn't change." Off by default; a new reel starts with it off, whatever the last reel had.
- **On:** the picture fades in from black over the clip's first 0.5 s and out to black over its last 0.5 s; a clip under 2 s fades over a quarter of its length at each end. The brightness at clip time *t* of a clip *L* long with fade *f* is min(1, *t* / *f*, (*L* − *t*) / *f*), at least 0. The sound is not faded.
- **Captions and branding fade with the picture:** the fade is applied after the overlay.
- **Render:** the render spec carries `fadeMs` (0, or absent, when off). Android adds a Media3 `RgbMatrix` after the `OverlayEffect` that scales red, green and blue by the brightness at each frame's clip time (`clipTimeMs`, §6.3.2). iOS scales them with `CIColorMatrix` in `FrameComposer` after the overlay is composited, at the frame's own time.
- **Preview:** the Lyrics player shows the fade, as a black layer over the reel whose opacity is 1 − brightness at the playhead. The Trim tab's player plays the raw video and doesn't.
- **Kept:** with the fine-tune (FR-24, `fadeEnds`; a file without it reads as off), and through a start-over.

**FR-34 Undo on Lyrics (M39).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-11): every change to the lines can be taken back.
- **Where:** Undo and Redo icon buttons (compact) on the right of Lyrics' app bar, greyed when there is nothing to undo or redo. Found by the owner on the phone (2026-11-11): beside the step label they squeezed the one-line title smaller, so on both Fine-tune steps the step label ("Step 1 of 2", "Step 2 of 2") now sits under the title in small muted text, and the title keeps its size.
- **Steps:** one per change to the lines: a saved text edit (not each letter), Add line, Delete, a drag, and each tap-to-time. An edit that changes no line (an ignored tap) is not a step. At most 50 steps; the oldest go first. A change after an undo clears Redo.
- **Quick message:** after a Delete, "Line deleted", and after a tap-to-time, "Line timed", each with **Undo**, for 4 s. The next change hides it.
- **A step back is a change:** the caption refills and the script is saved as `edited` after 1 s (FR-21), and the kept fine-tune follows (FR-24).
- **Back to before the first edit:** the lines become unedited, so they follow the written script as it is now: lines a run wrote meanwhile appear, and a run still writing keeps adding them.
- **Lifetime:** while Lyrics is open, Export and Cancel back included. Leaving Lyrics (back to Video, or Fine-tune closing) clears it; it is not kept for Continue later.
- **Not steps:** the Show lyrics switch, the Style sheet, Type them myself and Get again. Get again (Get, or Replace) clears the history.

**FR-35 Link in (M44, Android only).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-12, M43–M47 design): a long video from a link, to cut one topic out of (FR-36, FR-37). iOS shows none of this. Since M70 links come from any site yt-dlp reads, and the copy, links, identity, share-in and errors below are as FR-50 changes them.
- **Home:** the New reel card becomes two cards side by side, **From my videos** ("Choose or share a trimmed video", opens Source) and **From a YouTube link** ("Pick moments from a long video" since M53, *Claude's call*; "Find a topic in a long video" before; opens From a link). iOS keeps the one New reel card. (Asked by the owner, 2026-11-12: until then the link was a section on Source.)
- **From a link screen** (`/link`, title "From a link"): **YouTube link**, a text field "Paste a YouTube link" with a Paste button inside it, and **Read link**. Reading shows a card like the video card: YouTube's thumbnail, the title (two lines), the channel and the length, or the §8 message. Topic, length and Find the moments follow (FR-36).
- **Links:** YouTube watch, `youtu.be`, Shorts and live links (the M7 parser, `youtube_link.dart`). Other sites are not accepted in M44 (*Claude's call*; the design allowed them unpromised).
- **Reading:** yt-dlp's `-J` (no download) without JavaScript first, then with QuickJS when the quick run fails or finds no formats (§10 item 4). The facts kept: video ID, title, channel, length, thumbnail URL. The thumbnail is fetched by Flutter's image loader from YouTube's image server, the only network use outside Groq and yt-dlp.
- **Share in:** a `text/plain` share holding a YouTube link (from the YouTube app's Share, or a browser) opens From a link with the link filled in and read, asking first when it would close a reel, as FR-1 does for a video: "Start a new reel from this link? The one you're making will close." Text without a YouTube link is ignored.
- **Downloader in Settings** (`/settings/downloader`, FR-31's list, between Background work and Support): "Downloader", subtitle "yt-dlp <version>, reads video links". The screen shows the yt-dlp version, "Last checked <date>", and **Update downloader** (a spinner while it runs, then "Updated to <version>" or "Already up to date", or the §8 offline message). A hint: "YouTube changes often, so the downloader checks for a new yt-dlp once a week."
- **Weekly check:** at start-up on Android, when the last successful check is 7 days old or there never was one, yt-dlp updates itself quietly (about 3 MB). A failed check isn't recorded, so the next start tries again. It is the only code fetched at runtime (§9). The F-Droid build (M47) has no weekly check (`--dart-define=WEEKLY_YTDLP_UPDATES=false`, as F-Droid's build of Seal); Update downloader still works.
- **APK:** youtubedl-android brings Python, QuickJS and its native libraries, stored uncompressed (legacy JNI packaging) because it unpacks Python from the installed library folder.

**FR-36 Find the topic (M45, Android only).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-12, M43–M47 design): type a topic and a length, and the app finds the moments of a linked video (FR-35) about it, for the user to review before anything big is downloaded.
- **On From a link, under the link card** (once a link is read): **Topic** (a text field, any language: "What should the clip be about?"), **Length** (chips 15 s, 30 s, 60 s (default), 90 s, Custom; Custom asks for seconds from 5 to 300), **Output language** (a drop-down of Urdu (default), Arabic, Punjabi, Persian, Hindi, Bengali, English; asked by the owner, 2026-11-12) and **Find the moments**, which opens Review cuts (`/link/cuts`).
- **Transcript, cheapest first:**
  1. YouTube's captions, **only when the track is in the output language** (changed in M50, the owner, 2026-11-13: "leave the script if it exists in another language"; Hindi captions of an Urdu talk were unreadable in Add a moment): an uploaded track first (in the video's language when YouTube names one, else any), else the auto-generated track in the spoken language (`<code>-orig`), never a machine translation. Fetched as `json3` from the URL yt-dlp gives. The track's language is its code before any `-` (`hi-orig` is Hindi).
  2. Otherwise the audio only (`bestaudio[ext=m4a]`) is downloaded by yt-dlp into `sources/v_yt_<YouTube ID>/source.m4a` and written by the script pipeline as a video with that ID (FR-26, FR-12a, FR-14): cleaned, sent in pieces, resumed per piece, saved. Since M50 Groq is told the output language rather than detecting it, so an Urdu talk comes back in Urdu script (the owner's choice). A talk spoken in another language than the output one is then written as Whisper renders it into that language, which may be rough (*Claude's note*); the moments' words are translated by the topic search either way.
  - **Length caps:** up to 3 hours with captions in the output language, 2 hours without (§8 "too long"). Without them and over 1 hour, Review cuts asks first: "This video has no captions in Urdu, so Groq writes its script first. That takes a while and uses much of Groq's free daily audio." (the output language's name; "in Urdu" since M50) with Continue and Back. Not asked when the script in the output language is already saved, since nothing is sent (fixed in M54 task 10, owner, 2026-11-13).
- **Blocks:** transcript lines are merged into blocks of at least 5 s (a block ends at the first line end after 5 s), numbered. Cuts are runs of whole blocks, so a cut never starts or stops mid-line.
- **Reading in parts (removed in M53, FR-43):** blocks go to Groq's chat model (`openai/gpt-oss-120b`, low reasoning, JSON reply; §10 item 11) in parts of at most 20,000 UTF-8 bytes (4,000–5,000 tokens), with the topic; moments are asked to last 8–30 s (about a paragraph). Each part replies with candidate moments: first and last block, a score from 0 to 1, a one-line reason and the moment's words, both in the output language (Urdu asked for in Nastaliq script, not Hindi script; Punjabi in Shahmukhi), translated when spoken in another language. One moment adds about 500 reply tokens (§10 item 11). Progress: "Reading the transcript, part 3 of 6".
- **Every moment is shown, none ticked (changed with the owner, 2026-11-12; removed in M53, FR-43):** Review cuts lists every candidate in video order; of two that overlap the higher score stays, and those under 2 s are dropped. Nothing is ticked: the user ticks the moments the clip should have. There is no picking call (until then a second call ordered the candidates and the app fitted the best to 90–110 % of the length, which silently dropped good moments when they added up to more).
- **Saved:** the transcript of a linked video and (removed in M53) each topic's candidates (per topic and output language) are saved per YouTube ID (`topics/<YouTube ID>/`), so Find again or the same topic later sends nothing. Saved for 90 days like scripts (FR-14).
- **Groq's limits (removed in M53 with the search; the script pipeline waits out its own limits):** a 429 within a minute is waited out by the client as today. A longer one shows "Groq's free limit reached, continuing at 3:40" with Cancel and resumes on its own; a wait over 15 minutes is taken as the daily limit (§8), and the saved candidates stay.
- **Review cuts screen** (`/link/cuts`, title "Review cuts"):
  - While finding: the step ("Getting the captions", "Writing the script" with the script pipeline's progress; "Reading the transcript, part 3 of 6" and "Picking the best moments" removed in M53), a bar, and Cancel.
  - One card per moment: a checkbox, `1:02:13–1:02:31 · 18 s`, its words in the output language (three lines, tap to expand; a moment added from the transcript keeps the transcript's words), the reason in small text (removed in M53, FR-43), each in its script's font and direction (`ScriptText`). Its start and end are trimmed in the Play sheet (FR-39, M49; until then − and + on the card, removed at the owner's request, 2026-11-13): 0.5 s a tap, never under 2 s or outside the video, and a ticked cut never into another ticked one. Ticking a moment that overlaps a ticked one does nothing. Cards can be dragged to reorder; the clip takes the ticked ones in the list's order.
  - **Add a moment:** a sheet with the transcript blocks and a search field. Since M50 (the owner, 2026-11-13: "can I group lines?"), a moment spans several blocks: tap the first block, then the last, and every block between is selected; an **Add 1:02:13–1:02:31 · 18 s** button at the bottom adds the span as one moment, ticked, in video order (not when it overlaps a ticked one). A tap after the first block moves the end there (inside the span too); a tap before it starts again from there. A first tap made while searching clears the search and keeps that block in view, so the last is picked in context. Until M50 a tap added its one block at once.
  - **Footer** (kept short so the moments have the screen; changed after the phone check, 2026-11-12): the ticked total against the length ("58 s of 60 s"; over it, "74 s of 60 s · 14 s over" in the error colour: going over is allowed, the length is a target), a **Transition** button naming the chosen one (it opens a sheet with the four previews, two by two), and **Make the clip** (FR-37, M46).
  - **Transition picker:** Cut, Crossfade (default), Fade through black, Slide, as four tiles each looping a small preview (two coloured panels changing in that way). One transition for every join, 0.3 s.
  - **Find again** (removed in M53 with the search; the failure view's button reads Try again) in the app bar (until M49; replaced there by Update words, FR-40, and kept on the failure view): reads the transcript again with the same topic, asking "Replace your edited cuts?" first when cuts were changed. Since the topic's candidates are saved, it sends nothing and starts again from the list with nothing ticked.
  - **Play** on a card (M46, FR-37) downloads that stretch at up to 480p and plays it.
- **Consent (FR-30; the topic removed in M53):** finding sends the transcript text and the topic to Groq, so the consent screen says so, and a user who agreed before M45 is asked once more.

**FR-37 Download and join (M46, Android only).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-12, M43–M47 design): only the ticked moments download, and they are joined with the chosen transition into one video that goes on to Fine-tune. Since M70 streams may come from other sites with their own headers, and a site without parts may give the whole video after asking (FR-50).
- **Make the clip** (Review cuts' footer, on once a moment is ticked): the list gives way to the making view: "Downloading cut 2 of 4", "3.1 MB of 7.8 MB · 1.2 MB/s" and a bar, then "Joining the cuts" with its bar, and Cancel (one moment is kept without a join since M56, FR-46). Cancel stops and returns to the list; finished cuts are kept, so Make the clip again downloads only the rest. A failure shows its §8 message with Try again and Back.
- **Streams:** read again with yt-dlp each time (quick, then QuickJS, as FR-35 reads), since stream links expire after some hours. Video: H.264 MP4 at the best height up to 1080p; otherwise another MP4 (AV1) at the best height up to 1080p; failing both, the lowest MP4 above 1080p. Audio: the original track's `m4a` with the highest bitrate, a regular one before a "DRC" one. WebM streams (VP9, Opus) have no segment index and are not used; without an MP4 stream, §8 "can't be downloaded in parts". (Checked from the Mac, 2026-11-12: `UTHXU2dOO6Y` lists H.264, AV1 and VP9 at each height up to 1080p; its segments are 5 s of video, 10 s of audio.)
- **Only the cuts:** each ticked cut with a margin of the transition's length (0.3 s; none for Cut) on both sides, clamped to the video. From each stream's segment index (`sidx`, in its first bytes) the app fetches the header and the run of segments from the one holding the margin's start, which begins on a keyframe, to the one holding its end, by HTTP range requests (Dart `HttpClient`). A 403 (an expired link) reads the streams once more. The phone's yt-dlp has no FFmpeg, so `--download-sections` is not used (§10 item 10).
- **One file per cut:** `ffmpeg_kit` stream-copies the two downloads into one MP4, each stream delayed so they line up (M7's clip mux); the file's time zero is the earlier stream's first segment start. Cut files stay in the cache (`cuts/<YouTube ID>/`) with a small JSON beside each (the streams, the window, the file's start, what it covers) and are reused while the cache lasts.
- **Join (Media3):** one H.264/AAC MP4 at the downloaded size, at most 30 fps (YouTube's 1080p is often 60 fps; frames are dropped evenly), and a high bitrate (5 bits a pixel a second, about 10 Mbit/s at 1080p), in the list's order, each cut clipped from its own file at exact times. The encoder is asked for real time at 30 fps, not Media3's default top speed, which reserves the whole video chip (task 13).
  - **Timeline:** a transition sits in the margins: the outgoing cut plays on past its end while the incoming one starts before its start, the two overlapping for 0.3 s, so no word inside a cut is faded and the clip is the cuts' total plus 0.3 s a join (Cut adds nothing). A join whose margin is shortened by the video's start or end gets a shorter transition.
  - **Transitions:** Crossfade blends picture and sound across the 0.3 s. Fade through black fades the outgoing picture and sound out over the first half and the incoming in over the second. Slide pushes the incoming cut in from the right as the outgoing leaves to the left, with the sound crossfading. Cut joins back to back.
  - **How** (*Claude's call*): two layers in one Media3 composition. The upper plays each cut with its tail, without gaps; the lower plays only each cut's lead, at its transition, with black, silent gaps between. The upper's opacity and position, and both layers' volume, follow the transition; elsewhere the upper covers the lower. Both layers decode in software first, leaving the video chip to the encoder: on the Redmi Note 11 one hardware 1080p60 decode left no room for a second decoder, nor even for creating the hardware encoder (NO_MEMORY, 2026-11-12, task 13). Software decoding costs time, not quality.
  - **AV1:** the join decodes it as M31's convert does (Android 10 and later), so the joined file is H.264 either way and needs no separate convert; a phone that can't shows the §8 AV1 row's message.
- **Kept:** the joined file is imported like a shared video (FR-1): fingerprinted and kept as `sources/<videoId>/source.mp4`, named after the topic. Beside it, `link.json` keeps the YouTube ID, title, channel, topic, output language, transition and the join times. Fine-tune · Video then opens on it with the clip the whole video.
- **Join points are scenes** (FR-27): the join times (the middle of each transition) are scene edges for the crop, with what `scdet` finds. A detected cut within 0.5 s of a join gives way to it; the 0.5 s rule never removes a join, except one within 0.5 s of the clip's ends after a trim (that scene would be a blink). The crop's Cut keyframe sits at the join time, less `cutKeyframeLead` as for any cut.
- **In the background** (FR-16, added on the owner's question, 2026-11-12): Make the clip runs under the foreground service with its own notification: the step ("Reading the video's streams", "Downloading cut 2 of 4", "Joining the cuts", "Keeping the clip"), the topic and an overall percent (downloads 80 %, the join 20 %). When the app isn't on screen as it ends: "Clip ready", "Tap to crop it.", or "Clip stopped" with the §8 message. Review cuts carries on to Fine-tune meanwhile, so the app opens there. Cancel, or leaving Review cuts, ends the notification quietly. Play on a card is not kept in the background.
- **Recent:** like any video (FR-24): named after the topic, reopening Fine-tune on the joined file.
- **Play on a card:** a play button on each card downloads that stretch (no margin) at up to 480p (H.264 first) into the cache as a cut is, then plays it in a sheet (play, pause, a seek bar). While it downloads, the button shows a spinner. A played stretch is reused.
- **Lyrics in the output language:** see the M46 design in §12: Groq writes the joined clip's script, then its chat model translates the lines into the output language keeping their times, and only the translated lines exist from then on, edited as any lines (FR-21), drawn in the font for their script (§6.5).
  - **On Lyrics:** the Get card says "Lyrics in Urdu, chosen on From a link" in place of the Language dropdown. The run's steps gain "Translating into Urdu" ("Groq, in the cloud; only the translation is kept") before Lining up timestamps, and its language step reads as automatic. No line shows until the translation is in.
  - **Saved:** under the output language with source `groq-translated` (FR-14); the spoken script (`auto`) is deleted once translated, so the video reopens in one language. Edits save under the output language as `edited`, as for any script.
  - **Get again** (*Claude's call*): its language dialog stays. On a link clip another language translates into that one; Automatic gives the lines as spoken. When the written script's inferred language (§6.4) already is the one asked for, nothing is sent for translation.
  - **Failure:** a reply without every line, non-empty, is §8 "Groq couldn't translate the lyrics."; the spoken script stays saved, so Retry only translates again.

**FR-38 Continue a link (M48, Android only).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-13): a link left before Make the clip had no row in Recent, so the search and the edits to the cuts were lost. Choices made with the owner (2026-11-13).
- **Kept from Review cuts:** a link session is kept from the moment Review cuts opens (Find on From a link), while the moments are still being found, and again on every change: the cuts found, a tick, a nudge, a new order, an added moment, the transition. One per linked video and search (topic and output language, as FR-36's saved moments): `topics/<YouTube ID>/session_<key>.json` (version 1), holding the request (the video's ID, title, channel, length, thumbnail and captions links, the topic, the clip length, the output language), the cuts with their ticks in the list's order (none until found), the transition, whether the cuts were edited, and when it was saved. It goes with the video's `topics/` folder after 90 days unused (FR-36).
- **Recent (FR-11):** a link session has a row with a link icon, the YouTube video's title, `<topic> · Review cuts · <d MMM>` (the date of the last save) and "Continue" on the right. Long-press offers Delete, which deletes the session only (the saved transcript and moments stay, so the same search sends nothing to Groq).
- **Continue** opens Review cuts on the same request without the long-script question (it was answered, or the script is saved). The moments are found again from the saved transcript and moments, nothing sent to Groq when the search had finished; then the kept cuts replace the found list, as left, with the transition. A session left while finding carries on finding (it may send to Groq what wasn't saved yet, after FR-30 consent as before). Find again still asks before replacing edited cuts.
- **Spent by Make the clip:** once the clip is joined and kept (FR-37), the session is deleted; the joined clip's own fine-tune row ("From a link · Fine-tune …", FR-24) takes its place.

**FR-39 Trim while playing (M49, Android only).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-13): "fine tune clips required while playing mode to see what part is now included". Choices made with the owner from mockups (2026-11-13).
- **Play on a card** (FR-37) downloads the moment with 5 s before and after it (clamped to the video), at up to 480p as before (the owner chose 5 s, 2026-11-13).
- **The sheet's bar** covers that whole stretch: the included part in the accent colour, before and after it grey, with the playhead; a tap or drag seeks. While the playhead is outside the included part, the picture is dimmed with "Not in the clip" over it.
- **Start − + and End − +** under the bar, each with its exact time to a tenth of a second, move the edge by 0.5 s under the card's rules (FR-36: never under 2 s, never outside the video, a ticked cut never into another ticked one). A nudge changes the card's times at once and counts as an edit (Find again asks first; the session is kept, FR-38). The sheet is the only place to trim: the card's own − and + were removed (the owner, 2026-11-13).
- **After a nudge** the player jumps to 2 s before the edge that moved and plays to 2 s past it, then pauses, so the new start or stop is heard (*Claude's call* on 2 s). A nudge past the downloaded stretch first downloads the moment again with its margins (a spinner over the picture).
- **Opening** plays from the included start and pauses at the included end. Play after a pause goes on from the playhead; at the end of the stretch it starts again at the included start.
- **Words:** the card's words stay as Groq wrote them; nothing is sent for a nudge and no transcript lines are shown (the owner: the Hindi-script transcript is of no use, and rewriting the words costs Groq).

**FR-40 Update words (M49, Android only).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-13), after asking what Find again does: "update script for clips with modified start and end instead". Choices made with the owner (2026-11-13).
- **The app bar's button** on Review cuts is **Update words** (in place of Find again, which is removed there; the failure view keeps its own Find again). It shows only while some moment needs words; while it runs, a spinner takes its place.
- **Which moments:** those whose start or end changed (in the Play sheet, FR-39) and those added from the transcript (whose words are the transcript's own, often Hindi script). Such a card says "Words not updated yet" under its words until updated. The mark is kept with the session (FR-38).
- **What is sent:** one Groq chat call (`topicModel`, text only, after FR-30's consent) for all those moments: for each, its exact start and end and the transcript blocks (about 5 s each) that overlap them; Groq writes only the words spoken in that span, in the output language (Urdu in Nastaliq script, Punjabi in Shahmukhi), translated when spoken in another. The reason stays. About 500 reply tokens a moment (§10 item 11).
- **On reply:** each moment's words are replaced and its mark cleared, unless its times changed again meanwhile (then it keeps the mark). Failures show as a snackbar with the §8 message; a missing consent asks for it, then sends.

**FR-41 Script first (M51, Android only).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-13): "can I continue without topic? decide later after looking at the script". Choices made with the owner from mockups (2026-11-13).
- **From a link:** the topic may be left empty; the button then reads **Get the script** (with a topic, Find the moments as before). Length and output language are asked as before.
- **The script:** fetched or written exactly as for a search (FR-36, M50: captions in the output language, else Groq from the audio), and saved per language; no chat call is made.
- **Review cuts without a topic** shows the script as the page: at the top a **Find moments about…** field; under it **Your moments (n)**, folded or open (*Claude's call*: open), with the moment cards (tick, Play, words, reason; long-press to reorder); then every block of the script, each with its time, where a tap on the first block and then the last chooses a run as in Add a moment (M50). While a run is chosen, a bar above the footer says **Add 0:18–0:37 · 19 s** with a clear (×); Add puts the run in as one ticked moment, marked for Update words (FR-40). The footer (total, transition, Make the clip) and Update words work as with a topic.
- **A topic later (removed in M53, FR-43):** the field runs the topic search on the saved script (only its chat calls; candidates saved per topic as FR-36), with its step and bar under the field and Cancel. Found moments join Your moments unticked, in video order; one that overlaps a moment already there is left out. "Couldn't find this topic" and Groq's errors show as a snackbar; a missing consent asks for it, then searches.
- **Names:** with no topic, the joined clip, its notification and Recent use the video's title (*Claude's call*), and the Continue row (FR-38) says "Script · Review cuts".
- **Continue:** a session without a topic reopens on the script page with its moments.

**FR-42 Louder sound (M52, Android only).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-13): "can there be boost audio option? for clips with low volume". The owner said "implement it" to Claude's proposal: one switch that sets the level automatically, with a limiter, Android first.
- **Switch:** "Make the sound louder" on Fine-tune · Video's Trim tab, under "Fade in and out", with the hint "Quiet sound is raised to a steady level, and loud peaks are held down. You hear it in the saved reel." Off by default; a new reel starts with it off. iOS doesn't show it (AVFoundation's audio mix can't go above full volume; *Claude's call*: Android first).
- **Measured at export:** before the render, FFmpeg measures the clip's integrated loudness (`loudnorm` with `print_format=json`, `input_i` in LUFS) over the clip's range only. The gain in dB is −14 LUFS less that loudness, held between 0 and +15 dB, so a loud clip is never made quieter and a very quiet one isn't raised into hiss. Under +0.5 dB, or a loudness of −70 LUFS or lower (silence), the reel renders as without the switch.
- **Per clip:** the switch belongs to each reel, and the gain comes from that clip's own range, so each reel gets its own level. A joined link clip (FR-37) is one video by then, measured as a whole.
- **No sound to measure:** a clip without a sound track, or a measurement that fails, renders without the boost rather than failing the export (*Claude's call*).
- **Render:** the render spec carries `soundGain`, the linear gain (absent when there is none). Android adds an audio processor, `LoudnessBoost`, that multiplies each sample by the gain and then runs a peak limiter (ceiling −1 dBFS, instant attack, 150 ms release), so peaks never clip.
- **Preview:** neither player plays it louder (`video_player` can't go above full volume); the hint says so.
- **Kept:** with the fine-tune (FR-24, `louderSound`; a file without it reads as off), and through a start-over.

**FR-43 Links without a topic (M53, Android only).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-13): "remove topic. let decide the clips. no short listing", on From a link and on Review cuts too. The point is to make several clips from one transcribed video, because writing the script from the audio is the costly Groq step. The user picks every moment from the script; nothing is shortlisted. Choices made with the owner (2026-11-13).
- **From a link:** the link card, **Output language** and **Get the script**. The Topic field, the Length chips and Find the moments are removed. Length only steered the topic search, so it goes with it (*Claude's call*). The long-script question (FR-36, over 1 hour without captions in the output language) stays.
- **Review cuts** is always the script page of FR-41, with **Search the script** (FR-28) in place of the **Find moments about…** field: Your moments (in a sheet from the footer since M56, FR-46), the script blocks, choosing a run, Add, the footer (total, transition, Make the clip) and Update words (FR-40). A card has no reason line, because a moment the user chose has none.
- **Groq:** only the script from the audio (FR-36's transcript order, M50) and Update words' chat call (FR-40) are sent. The topic search goes, with its code: reading in parts, candidates, overlap and score rules, "Couldn't find this topic", and its progress steps. `topicModel` stays for Update words. FR-36's search bullets and FR-41's "A topic later" become history and are marked "(removed in M53)".
- **Names:** the joined clip, its notification and History use the video's title (FR-41's rule, now the only one).
- **One session per linked video:** `topics/<YouTube ID>/session.json` (version 2), holding the request without topic or length (the video's ID, title, channel, length, thumbnail and captions links, the output language), the cuts, the transition, the edited flag and the save time. Get the script on a video that already has a session in another output language asks first (FR-44's question) and then replaces it.
- **One unfinished piece of work per linked video:** Make the clip deletes the session as before (FR-38), and the joined clip's fine-tune becomes the unfinished work. Starting again on that video (New in History, or Get the script from From a link) asks first, then deletes the session and any unfinished joined clip of that video: its fine-tune and its kept `sources/<videoId>/` file. Reels already made stay.
- **Old data, removed once at the first start after the update:** version 1 sessions (`session_<key>.json`) and saved topic candidates (`topic_<key>_<transcript>.json`) under `topics/`. Saved transcripts (`transcript_<language>.json`, and the `v_yt_` scripts of FR-14) stay: they are the costly part.

**FR-44 History (M54).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-13): "i need to reuse processed youtube video for other clips… a more polished history section". The owner chose from mockups a separate History screen with a row per video that folds open (Home B: only unfinished work stays on Home), and grouping for both linked and local videos. This replaces FR-11's list on Home; FR-11's row words carry over. Since M71 a kept video's moments, sessions and joined clips group and act as a link's do (FR-51).
- **Home:** the Recent list leaves Home. Under the New reel cards:
  - **Carry on** lists unfinished work only: link sessions (FR-38) and kept fine-tunes (FR-24), newest first, at most 3, each row as today with Continue. With nothing unfinished, the heading and rows are hidden (*Claude's call*).
  - **History ›** is a row that is always shown and opens History.
- **History screen** (`/history`, title "History"): chips **All**, **Links** and **My videos**. iOS shows no chips, because it has no links. The screen has one group per video, ordered by its latest activity (a reel made, a script saved, or unfinished work saved). Empty, it reads "Videos you make reels from will appear here."
  - **What counts as one video:** a linked video is grouped by YouTube ID, and its `v_yt_<ID>` script and every clip joined from it fold into its group. A local video is grouped by fingerprint (FR-1). A joined link clip never shows as a video of its own.
  - **Parent row:** thumbnail (YouTube's for a link, the video's frame for a local one), title, then `<length> · script saved · <n> reels`, showing only the parts that exist, with a fold arrow and **New** on the right. A tap folds or unfolds the row. All rows open folded (*Claude's call*).
  - **First child, when there is unfinished work:** "Review cuts · <n> moments" or "Fine-tune", then "Left <d MMM>", with **Continue**. It continues as FR-38 and FR-24 do.
  - **Other children:** the video's reels, newest first, each with a thumbnail, its name, length and date, and one chip per source range it used (`2:14–2:40`) in source order. Tap opens Done; long-press offers Delete (FR-11).
  - **New** on a link opens Review cuts on the saved script in its language, with no moments. With no saved script it writes one, as Get the script does. New on a local video opens Fine-tune · Video on the kept file, and the saved script is reused (FR-14). If the kept file is gone, the row says "Choose or share the video again" with the §8 "Video not found" message; sharing it again finds the script by fingerprint.
  - **Replacing unfinished work:** before New, or anything else that would replace a video's unfinished work, the app asks "Start new clips from this video? The unfinished one will be deleted." with **Start** and **Keep**.
  - **Long-press a parent row** offers Delete video, which asks first and then deletes the video's saved scripts (every language, partial runs included), its unfinished work, its kept files, its cut downloads, and its reels in the app (MP4 and thumbnail). Copies saved to the gallery stay.
- **Where a reel came from:** each new reel records `sourceVideoId` (the fingerprint it was rendered from), `youtubeId` (for a joined link clip) and `usedRanges` (start and end in the source, in ms).
  - For a local video, the one range is the clip's start and end.
  - For a joined link clip, `link.json` also keeps each cut's source start and end and where it starts in the joined file. A reel's ranges are the part of each cut inside its trim, mapped back to the YouTube video, without the transition margins. A clip joined before M54 has no cut times, so its reels are grouped but have no chips (*Claude's call*).
- **Used marks on Review cuts:** script blocks that overlap a range used by a reel of this video are tinted and tagged "In <reel name>", naming the newest such reel. A moment card in Your moments that overlaps gets the same tag (*Claude's call*). This is a mark only; a used block can still be chosen. Local videos have no mark, only the chips in History (*Claude's call*).
- **Retention:** saved scripts and linked transcripts no longer expire. FR-14's 90 days and 200 videos no longer apply to them; they go only with Delete video. Kept files (a local video's copy, a link's `source.m4a`, cut downloads) still go after 90 days unused; since M57 a link's `source.m4a` and its pieces go as soon as its transcript is saved (FR-47). Reels: at most 50, as FR-11.
- **Old data, removed once at the first start after the update:** reels with no recorded source, meaning the record, MP4 and thumbnail (the owner: no "Older reels" section). Copies saved to the gallery stay.

**FR-28 Search the script (M53, Android only).** Reserved on 2026-11-05 and placed here. Asked by the owner (2026-11-13), once the topic search was removed: "will need it to look for context". The owner chose a word search on the phone over a meaning search through Groq.
- **Where:** at the top of Review cuts' script page (FR-41, FR-43), in place of Find moments about…, as a field "Search the script" with a clear (×).
- **As you type:** blocks holding a match are found on the phone, and nothing is sent. Each match is highlighted in its block. "3 of 12" shows beside ˄ and ˅, which scroll to the previous or next match and mark it as current. With no match: "No match". An empty field shows the whole script with no highlights.
- **Matching:** the words typed, as one phrase, inside a block's text. Case is ignored. Arabic-script variants count as one letter (ی ي ى, ک ك, ہ ه ھ, ۃ ة, أ إ آ ا), and diacritics (harakat, the tatweel ـ) are ignored on both sides (*Claude's call*). Whole words are not required, so part of a word matches.
- **Finding, not choosing:** a search changes nothing. Choosing a run (tap the first block, then the last), Add and the "In <reel name>" marks (FR-44) work as without it; the field keeps its text while a run is chosen.
- **Not kept:** the search text isn't saved with the session (*Claude's call*).
- **Placement and typing** (*Claude's call*, M53): the field stays fixed above the scrolling page, so ˄ ˅ are always in reach; as the user types, the first match becomes current and is scrolled into view. A match's highlight is a background only, so Nastaliq letters keep their joins.

**FR-45 Groq allowance (M55).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-13): "can groq remaining be viewed?", then "in the app". Choices made with the owner (2026-11-13): shown in Settings and beside Get, a warning that can be passed, no check button. Changed the same day (M55 task 7): Groq's replies carry no audio figures (§10 item 5), so the owner chose to count on the phone.
- **Counted on the phone:** each script piece Groq writes adds its length (at least 10 s, as Groq bills) with the time it was sent; sends older than a day are dropped. Left this hour is 120 min less the audio sent in the last 60 min; left today is 8 h less the last 24 h (Groq's free plan for `whisper-large-v3`). Kept in the phone's settings, apart from the key. Audio sent with the same key from another phone or app isn't counted, and a paid key still shows free-plan figures, so the text says "About" and "Counted on this phone, free plan". Saving a key (the check passing) or Remove clears the count.
- **Groq's refusal:** when Groq refuses a piece for its limit (429), both figures read 0 until its `retry-after` wait ends, and the text says "Groq's limit is reached until 11:20".
- **Settings:** under "Key works", two bars, "This hour" with "41 min of 2 h left" and "Today" with "6 h 20 min of 8 h left", each filled by the share left, then the hint "Estimates, counted on this phone (free plan)". A bar below 15 % left turns to the error colour. Amounts are whole minutes, rounded down (owner, 2026-11-13: "pictorial / progress bar representation").
- **Beside Get:** under Get lyrics from Groq (Fine-tune · Lyrics) and Get the script (From a link), when a key is saved: the bar of the allowance that runs out first, and "About 41 min of Groq audio left this hour", or "… left today" when the day's allowance runs out first.
- **Before a script:** when the audio still to send is more than what is left of the allowance that runs out first, the app asks "Not enough Groq time left": "This needs about 52 min of audio; about 41 min are left this hour. Groq stops when it runs out. The lines written so far are kept, and getting the script again carries on from there later." with **Not now** and **Go on**.
  - **Audio still to send:** on Fine-tune · Lyrics, nothing for Get when the script in that language is saved; otherwise the video's length less the pieces a partial run already wrote (FR-14). Get again counts the same way, since it resumes a partial run too.
  - **On Review cuts** (a link without captions whose script isn't saved), after the long-script question: the whole video's length (*Claude's call*: the link's audio and its partial run are found only after the download, so they aren't read here). A continued session is asked too, though it skips the long-script question, since the allowance may have run low since (*Claude's call*).

**FR-46 One moment, and Your moments in a sheet (M56, Android only).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-13): "why joining the cuts when one clip? my moments position? too much scrolling to reach from middle of script". Choices made with the owner from mockups (2026-11-13): skip the re-encode for one moment, and a Moments button in the footer opening the cards in a sheet.
- **One moment, no join:** Make the clip with one ticked moment downloads it without the transition's margins (there is nothing to blend into) and keeps the downloaded file as it is, with no re-encode. Its steps are "Reading the video's streams", "Downloading the cut" with its bar and amount, and "Keeping the clip". The notification's percent is all download.
  - **The file is longer than the moment:** it runs from the segment holding the moment's start, which begins on a keyframe, to the one holding its end (FR-37), so a few seconds past the moment on each side. Fine-tune · Video opens with the clip set to the moment's place in the file, so the Trim tab's nudges can reach into those seconds, and the export trims as for any clip.
  - **The link's facts** (FR-37, FR-44): no join times, and one cut whose joined start is the moment's place in the file, so a reel's used ranges map back as for a joined clip.
  - **The cut stays in the cache** (*Claude's call*): the file is copied for keeping, so Make the clip again, or with more moments, reuses it.
  - **An AV1 picture** (no H.264 stream, FR-37) still goes through the native joiner, which writes H.264, as a kept AV1 video would be converted anyway (M31). The step reads "Converting the cut" (*Claude's call*).
  - Two or more moments are joined as before.
- **Your moments in a sheet:** the script page is the search bar and the script only. The footer's first row gets a **Moments (n)** button (n counts every moment, ticked or not) before the total and Transition. It opens a panel from the bottom over the script, up to three quarters of the screen, headed "Your moments (n)" with a close (×), holding the moment cards as before (tick, Play, words, long-press to reorder, the "In <reel name>" tag). With none it says "No moments yet. Tap the first line of a moment in the script, then the last."
  - The footer stays below the panel, so the total, Transition and Make the clip stay in reach.
  - The ×, Moments again, a tap on the shade over the script, or Back closes it; the script keeps its place. Make the clip closes it.
  - Adding a run doesn't open it; the count on Moments goes up (*Claude's call*).
  - The panel is part of Review cuts, not a route of its own, so a tick, a reorder or Play's download shows at once (*Claude's call*).

**FR-47 Free the storage nothing uses (M57).** Found by the owner (2026-11-16): the app took 2 GB on the phone while History showed three videos. Three leaks, besides the on-device Whisper model of before M10 (`files/ggml-medium.bin`, 539 MB), which the owner deleted by hand and no code removes (the owner: "do not add code").
- **A link's audio goes once its transcript is saved.** The whole audio (`sources/v_yt_<ID>/source.m4a`) and its cleaned pieces only serve the Groq transcript of FR-36. Once that transcript is saved under `topics/`, the folder is deleted; the `v_yt_` script under `transcripts/` stays. A transcript in another output language downloads the audio again (*Claude's call*: rare, and the audio was 30–50 MB a talk plus as much again in pieces).
- **A kept video nobody can reach goes after 7 days.** Until M57 a `sources/<videoId>/` folder was kept for 90 days whenever its `transcripts/<videoId>/` folder existed, and saving a video's title (on import) creates that folder. So a video imported and left without a script, a fine-tune or a reel had no History group (M54) yet kept its copy. Now a kept video counts as unused when its `transcripts/` folder holds nothing but its title and last-used time, and no reel records it as its source; such a folder is deleted when nothing in it has changed for 7 days. Its title-only `transcripts/` folder stays (*Claude's call*: a few bytes).
- **Not changed here:** a reel file whose record is gone from the reel list (one such 64 MB file was found and deleted by hand) is not looked for; M59 does that (FR-49).

**FR-48 Smaller reels (M58).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-16): "lower the reel bitrate to 4 Mbps". Reels were 56–70 MB a minute (8–9 Mbit/s): Android let the hardware encoder choose, and iOS asked for 0.12 bits a pixel a frame (about 7.5 Mbit/s at 1080 × 1920). WhatsApp compresses a status further anyway.
- **Rate:** the reel's video is asked for 4 Mbit/s on Android (Media3 `VideoEncoderSettings`) and iOS (`AVVideoAverageBitRateKey`), about 30 MB a minute with the sound. The encoder's rate mode stays its default (*Claude's call*).
- **Not changed:** the link join (FR-37) keeps its high rate, since the reel is encoded again from it; the AV1 conversion (M31) keeps Media3's default.

**FR-49 Files a killed render leaves (M59).** Found by the owner (2026-11-16): a reel deleted from History left a 10 MB MP4 behind. The app had been force-closed halfway through a render, so the half-written `<name>.mp4` and its caption PNGs stayed with no record; the render again on the next launch saw the name taken and wrote `<name>-2`-style, which was the reel later deleted.
- **At start-up,** after the reels without a source go (FR-44), every file in `documents/reels/` that no stored reel names (as its MP4 or thumbnail, readable or not) is deleted when it hasn't changed for an hour. The hour keeps a render that is still running in the background safe when the app's screen starts again (*Claude's call*).
- **Not changed:** a render cut short this way is not resumed or reported; the user renders again.

**FR-50 Links from other sites (M70, Android only).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-16): "Allow URLs other than Youtube, all YTLD supports", then "I wanted to support Facebook, Instagram links as well". Any link yt-dlp reads is accepted. Facebook and Instagram are the sites aimed at; others work as far as their streams allow. Changes FR-35's "Links" and FR-37's "Only the cuts" as below.
- **Links:** any `http` or `https` link with a host (`https://` is added when the scheme is missing). YouTube links are still read by the M7 parser into the video ID, so videos kept before M70 keep their folders, scripts and sessions. Other links go to yt-dlp as pasted. A page yt-dlp has no extractor for gets §8 "The downloader can't read videos from this site."
- **Identity:** a linked video keeps `pageUrl` (yt-dlp's `webpage_url`, else the pasted link) and `site` (yt-dlp's `extractor_key`, e.g. `Facebook`, `Instagram`, `Youtube`). Its key, which names its folders and records, is the YouTube ID for YouTube, else `<site in lower case>_<id>` with every character outside `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` replaced by `_`; a key over 64 characters is replaced by `<site>_` and the first 16 hex of the SHA-256 of the ID (*Claude's call*). Records kept before M70 have no `pageUrl` or `site`: they are YouTube, and the watch URL is built from the ID. JSON keys stay as they are (`id` in a kept linked video, `youtubeId` in `link.json`), so old files read unchanged; in Dart the field is `videoKey`.
- **yt-dlp:** `quickVideoInfo`, `videoInfo` and `downloadAudio` take the page URL, and Kotlin no longer builds a YouTube URL. The script's audio is `bestaudio[ext=m4a]/bestaudio/worst`: a site with no sound-only stream gives its smallest whole file, whose sound the script uses; it is deleted once the transcript is saved (FR-47).
- **Card:** for a site other than YouTube, the line under the title reads "<Site> · <channel>", or only the site when yt-dlp names no channel. The thumbnail comes from the site's image server, as YouTube's does.
- **Share in:** a `text/plain` share holding any `http` or `https` link (the first one in the text) opens From a link and reads it, as FR-35 does for YouTube; Facebook's and Instagram's Share give text with the link.
- **Login walls:** yt-dlp errors that say a login, cookies or "rate-limit reached or login required" (Instagram often does) give §8 "This video needs a login to the site, which the app can't do. Try a public video." The app takes no cookies or accounts (*Claude's call*: signing in would mean handing the app a password or cookies).
- **Captions:** only YouTube's `json3` tracks are read (FR-36). On other sites Groq writes the script from the audio (M50).
- **Streams:** a format counts as range-fetchable when its protocol is `https` or `http`, or `http_dash_segments` without a `fragments` list (an unfragmented DASH file, as Facebook and Instagram list theirs). Each format's `http_headers` from yt-dlp go with every request for it, since a site's servers may check the User-Agent or Referer. YouTube's stream choice is unchanged.
- **Whole file when there are no parts:** when a video has no MP4 picture-and-sound pair with a segment index, but has an MP4 stream carrying both, Make the clip and Play on a card first ask "This site only offers the whole video (84 MB). Download it?" with **Download** and **Cancel**; "(size unknown)" replaces the size when neither yt-dlp's `filesize` or `filesize_approx` nor the server's `Content-Range` gives it. Download fetches that stream (H.264 first, the best height up to 1080p, as FR-37 picks video) whole into `cuts/<key>/whole.<formatId>.mp4`, showing the bytes as cuts do. It is kept as a cut covering the whole video (`CutFile`, time zero at 0), so every moment and Play reuse it, and FR-47 deletes it with the video's cuts. Cancel returns to the moments. The answer lasts while Review cuts is open (*Claude's call*). A video with neither (HLS only) keeps §8 "This video can't be downloaded in parts." This relaxes FR-37's "the whole video is never downloaded" for such sites only, at the owner's request, since Facebook and Instagram often serve a reel as one file.
- **Copy:** Home's card is "From a link" (subtitle unchanged); From a link's field is "Video link", hint "Paste a video link"; §8's messages drop "from YouTube"; the Downloader hint reads "Video sites change often, so the downloader checks for a new yt-dlp once a week."
- **Not changed:** YouTube's path; iOS has no link input.

**FR-51 Moments from my videos (M71, Android only).** Asked by the owner (2026-11-16): "For Local Video, start same flow: lyrics > moment > fine tuning". The owner chose (2026-11-16) to keep a way straight to Fine-tune for a video that is already trimmed, and to leave iOS as it is. A video from the gallery or a share now goes through the link flow's script page and moments (FR-43, FR-28, FR-39, FR-40, FR-46, FR-37), with nothing downloaded.
- **Source (Android):** once a video is kept, the video card is followed by **Output language** (FR-36's list; the last one used is preselected) and **Get the script** (filled), which opens Review cuts for that video, and a text button **Use the whole video**, which opens Fine-tune as before M71. A shared video (FR-1) now opens Source with its card instead of Fine-tune, so the choice is offered (*Claude's call*). iOS keeps its flow.
- **Review cuts for a local video:** the same screen, titled with the video's title. Getting the script runs the script pipeline on the kept file (cleaning, pieces, FR-30 consent, FR-45 allowance) in the output language, with no download and no captions. A saved script for that video and language (FR-14) opens without sending anything.
- **Moment source:** Review cuts, its saved session (FR-38) and the clip record (`link.json`, FR-37) take a source that is either a linked video or a kept local video. A local source is saved as `{"kind": "local", "videoId": "v_…"}`; a record without `kind` is a link, so files kept before M71 read unchanged (*Claude's call*: one record type, so Lyrics and History treat both alike).
- **Make the clip:** nothing downloads; each cut is the kept file itself (a `CutFile` over the whole video, time zero at 0). One moment opens Fine-tune on the kept file at the moment, with no copy (FR-46). Several are joined as FR-37 joins (transition, margins, 30 fps, join points as scenes), and the joined file is kept as a new video, named after the source video's title. The making view skips "Reading the video's streams" and "Downloading".
- **Play on a card:** plays the kept file from the moment's start to its end; nothing is cut.
- **Lyrics:** as for a linked clip (FR-37): in the output language chosen on Source; the Get card says "Lyrics in Urdu, chosen on Source".
- **Continue and History (FR-38, FR-44):** a local video left on Review cuts gets a Carry on row as a link does. Its History row works as a link's (owner, 2026-11-16: "Same history for local as link video"): New opens Review cuts on its saved script in that language with no moments, after asking when unfinished work would go (FR-44); Continue reopens the session; the reels joined from it group under it. This replaces New opening Source's card. Use the whole video stays on Source.
- **In the background (FR-16):** as for links.
- **Not changed:** iOS (no Review cuts and no join there); a video shared while a reel is open asks first (FR-1).

## 4. Non-functional requirements

- **Platforms:** Android (minSdk 24) and iOS (deployment target 15.5, set for ML Kit; kept after ML Kit went in M47, since nothing is gained by lowering it). iOS is built in M26. Store builds become possible (§9, M27).
- **Responsiveness:** transcription, FFmpeg, waveform peaks and file I/O never block the UI isolate. Every long step shows progress and can be cancelled.
- **Storage (changed in M23):**
  - Kept videos: `sources/<videoId>/source.<ext>` in app support, the file the user shared or picked, kept once (FR-1); for an AV1 video, its H.264 copy `source.h264.mp4` instead (M31). It stays while its script or fine-tune is kept (FR-14 retention) and is deleted with them; imported but never scripted, it is deleted after 7 days (since M57 also when only its title is kept and no reel was made from it, FR-47). Saving a video's title counts as a use, so a video just imported isn't pruned. Cleaned audio pieces (FR-26) are cached beside it.
  - Saved scripts: `transcripts/` in app support (FR-14).
  - Unfinished fine-tunes: `draft-<requested>.json` beside the scripts in `transcripts/<videoId>/` (FR-24); they go with the folder.
  - Exports: `reels/` in app documents.
  - Copies and other files are written to `.part` files and renamed when complete.
  - Every start deletes folders whose name isn't a fingerprint ID (`v_` and 16 hex digits): old YouTube data.
- **Data use:** nothing is downloaded. The only upload is the cleaned script audio pieces (about 16 kHz mono FLAC), to Groq (the Fix with Groq lines went in M36).
- **Privacy (rewritten in M60):** nothing of the user's leaves the device except, after FR-30's consent, what Groq needs (the cleaned audio pieces of FR-12a, and the script text of FR-37's translation and FR-40's Update words), and, for a link (Android), yt-dlp's requests to the video's site for the parts kept and its weekly update from GitHub (FR-35). The video itself never leaves the phone. No library in the app sends anything of its own since M47 (§9.1 is the published wording).
- **Large text (M34):** the phone's text size is followed up to **1.3×** and no further (`maxTextScale` in `app.dart`, `MediaQuery.withClampedTextScaling`). The owner chose a cap over unlimited growth (2026-11-10), so every screen keeps its layout; users at the largest system sizes get less than they asked for. The reel preview and the export draw captions at a fixed size with no text scaling at all (`reel_preview.dart`).
  - **App bar titles** stay on one line and shrink to the room left (`ScreenTitle`), next to the back button and the step label.
  - **Type hierarchy:** section labels 16 sp semi-bold (`SectionLabel`, also "Recent" on Home and "Groq API key"), body text 14 sp, list rows 14 sp with 12 sp muted subtitles (the theme's `listTileTheme`), hints 12 sp muted (`HintText`). A section label is never smaller than what it holds. Android 14 and later scale small text more than large text (12 sp and 14 sp both end near 24 at 200%), which is why labels and body are two steps apart.

## 5. Architecture

- **Folders:** feature-first, `lib/src/features/<feature>/{domain,data,application,presentation}`, with a layer only when it has code.
- **State:** Riverpod 3 (`Notifier`/`NotifierProvider`). The reel in progress is one immutable `ReelDraft` in `reelDraftProvider`. Changing the transcript replaces the lines and clears per-line caption edits (FR-21); the clip, its nudges and the crop keyframes stay.
- **Services:** small interfaces exposed as providers, so fakes can replace them in tests: `VideoSource` (from M23 the `LocalVideoImporter`, with `IncomingVideoSource` for shares), `Transcriber`, `CaptionTranslator`, `Renderer`, `RecentReelsRepository`, `BackgroundWorkPlatform`.
- **Speech to text:** `GroqTranscriber` (FR-12a) sends the pieces one by one and emits per-piece progress and lines.
- **Navigation:** `go_router`, with route paths in `AppRoutes`.
- **Rendering (Android):** Media3 Transformer behind the Kotlin channels `status_studio/render` (`start`, `cancel`) and `status_studio/render/events`.
  - Input: the clip file plus one transparent PNG, exactly the output size, holding caption and branding drawn by Flutter. It is applied last.
  - Media3 trims, crops or blurs, scales, overlays and encodes on the phone's hardware.
  - Preview and export share `cropRectFor` and `layoutOverlay`.
  - iOS (M26): AVFoundation behind the same channels and the same spec map: the clip is cut out of the video into an `AVMutableComposition`, `AVAssetReader` decodes it, Core Image draws each frame with the rectangle from the same `ndcRectAt` maths (ported to Swift), and `AVAssetWriter` encodes hardware H.264 and AAC. Not a custom `AVVideoCompositing` (see M26 task 4).
- **FFmpeg (changed in M23):** audio cleaning and splitting for Groq (FR-26), thumbnails, probing (FFprobe: duration, size, rotation, audio track) and sampling small JPEG frames for Follow the person (FR-23, FR-27). The mjpeg encoder is there; the PNG encoder is not (`--disable-zlib`). The bundled build can't encode H.264 or read https (§10 Q1).

### Package map

| Need | Package | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Video in (M23) | our own `status_studio/video_in` channel on Android (Kotlin) and iOS (Swift, fed by our Share Extension; `receive_sharing_intent` rejected on both, §10 item 9), `image_picker` | Share-in; `pickVideo` uses the Android Photo Picker and PHPicker, no permission. Replaced yt-dlp (removed in M23) |
| Speech to text (cloud) | `dart:io` `HttpClient` | Hand-built multipart to `api.groq.com`; `GET /openai/v1/models` checks a key |
| Secret storage | `flutter_secure_storage` | The Groq key only |
| FFmpeg | `ffmpeg_kit_flutter_new_min` ^2.x | The only FFmpeg (LGPL); no mediacodec, no TLS, no libx264 |
| Final render (Android; iOS M26) | Media3 `transformer`, `effect`, `common` 1.11.1 | `Transformer`, `ClippingConfiguration`, `Crop`, `Presentation`, `GaussianBlurWithFrameOverlaid`, `OverlayEffect` + `BitmapOverlay`. iOS: AVFoundation, `AVAssetWriter` and Core Image |
| Downloads (removed in M23) | | Nothing is downloaded any more. `HttpClient` stays only for Groq and the key check |
| Cut downloads (M46, Android) | `dart:io` `HttpClient`, `ffmpeg_kit_flutter_new_min`, Media3 `Composition` | Byte ranges from each MP4 stream's `sidx` (yt-dlp gives the links), stream-copied by FFmpeg into one file per cut, joined by Media3 with two sequences, `VideoCompositorSettings` and an audio processor per item (FR-37) |
| Faces (M20) | `google_mlkit_face_detection` | Fast mode, small faces allowed; bundled model |
| Faces (M47) | `tflite_flutter` and `assets/models/face_detection_full_range.tflite` (1.1 MB, Apache 2.0, MediaPipe BlazeFace full range; `assets/models/NOTICE.txt`) | `FaceModel` in its own `IsolateInterpreter`, 4 threads, 192 × 192 input; about 64 ms a frame on the Redmi with the JPEG decode (§10 item 12). Decoding in `style/domain/blaze_face.dart`. Replaced `google_mlkit_face_detection`. |
| Bodies (M25) | `tflite_flutter` 0.12.1 and `assets/models/efficientdet_lite0.tflite` (4.4 MB, Apache 2.0, TF Hub, the build with NMS) | In an `IsolateInterpreter`, 4 threads, 320 × 320 input; about 100–250 ms a frame on the Redmi (§10 item 7). `android/gradle.properties` sets `kotlin.jvm.target.validation.mode=warning` because the plugin's Kotlin and Java targets differ under this JDK. +11.4 MB of APK, accepted. iOS in M26. |
| Translation (removed in M30) | — | — |
| Preview / gallery / share | `video_player` / `gal` / `share_plus` | |
| Key-value storage | `shared_preferences`, with `shared_preferences_android` overridden to 2.4.26 | `recentReels` (JSON). 2.5.x unregisters its one shared `data_store` instance whenever any Flutter engine detaches, and the foreground service's engine does when a script or export ends, so every later read threw; 2.4.x unregisters per engine. The override and its comment in `pubspec.yaml` go when the plugin stops sharing it. |
| Saved models (M42) | `freezed`, `json_serializable`, `build_runner` (dev); `freezed_annotation`, `json_annotation` | Code generation at build time only; nothing ships that collects data, so §9.2 is unchanged. `build.yaml` sets `checked: true` |
| Background work | `flutter_foreground_task`, `flutter_local_notifications` | No TaskHandler: the work stays in the app's isolate. Notifications need core library desugaring |

### Engineering rules

- **Code generation for saved models (M42):** the four classes saved as JSON (`SourceVideo`, `SavedTranscript`, `PartialTranscript`, `RecentReel`) use `freezed` and `json_serializable`; run `fvm dart run build_runner build -d` after changing one, and commit the generated `*.freezed.dart` and `*.g.dart` files. The saved JSON keeps its pre-M42 shape, so files and preferences written before still read: durations as whole milliseconds under `…Ms` keys, and a script's `languageCode` and `lines` and a reel's `width` and `height` at the top level (these two models write `toJson` by hand, because `json_serializable` can't flatten a field). `json_serializable` runs `checked`, so a malformed file throws `CheckedFromJsonException` (an `Exception`), never a `TypeError`. Every other class writes `copyWith`, `==` and `hashCode` by hand.
- **Copy:** literal from this document and the canvas. The app is general-purpose: never name a content type (naat, bayan…) in UI or code.
- **Import boundaries:**
  - Only `LocalVideoImporter` reads shared or picked files and copies them into app storage (M23). No yt-dlp or YouTube code remains.
  - Only `lib/src/core/ffmpeg/` imports FFmpeg Kit.
  - Only the Kotlin `render/` package imports Media3, and only the Swift `ios/Runner/Render/` folder imports AVFoundation and Core Image (M26).
  - The background plugins are reached only through `BackgroundWorkPlatform`.
  - Only `lib/src/features/style/data/` imports `tflite_flutter` (`FaceModel`, `BodyModel`, behind `OnDeviceDetector`). No library that sends data of its own (ML Kit, Play Services, Firebase) is added (M47).
- **Errors are typed:** `VideoUnreadableException` (no audio track, or unreadable, M23), `KeptVideoMissingException` (M23), `OfflineException`, `InsufficientStorageException`, `NoGroqKeyException`, `NoSpeechFoundException`, and so on. Screens map them to §8. A failing background-platform call is reported with `FlutterError.reportError` and never stops the work.
- **New dependencies:** maintained and widely downloaded on pub.dev. Add them with `fvm flutter pub add`.
- **Tests never touch the network or platform:**
  - Fake the incoming-video source, the picker, Groq and FFmpeg. Groq requests run against a loopback `HttpServer`.
  - Override `backgroundWorkPlatformProvider` with `FakeBackgroundWorkPlatform`.

## 6. Domain model and rules

### 6.1 Types (Dart)

```dart
class SourceVideo { String videoId; String title; Duration duration; } // M23: videoId is the fingerprint v_<16 hex>; title is the display name; no channel
enum ScriptLanguage { auto, urdu, arabic, punjabi, persian, hindi, bengali, english } // whisperCode ('auto','ur',…) and label
class TranscriptLine { int index; String text; Duration start; Duration end; }
class Transcript { String languageCode; List<TranscriptLine> lines; }
sealed class LineSelection { NoSelection, StartSelected(int start), RangeSelected(int start, int end) } // M23: always the whole script (FR-4 removed)
class ClipRange { Duration start; Duration end; } // M23: defaults to 0..duration
enum AspectPreset { story9x16, portrait4x5, square1x1, landscape16x9, free }
enum FillMode { crop, blur }  enum CropFocus { left, center, right }
class FreeCropRect { double left, top, width, height; } // fractions of the source frame
class FrameSettings { AspectPreset preset; FillMode fill; CropAnchor anchor; FreeCropRect freeCrop; CropPath cropPath; } // anchor replaces CropFocus (M19); Left/Center/Right set anchor.x
class CropAnchor { double x, y; }                                    // 0..1 across the window's room to move (FR-22)
enum CropArrival { glide, cut }
class CropKeyframe { Duration time; CropAnchor anchor; CropArrival arrival; } // video time
class CropPath { List<CropKeyframe> keyframes; }                      // empty = a fixed crop
class OutputSize { int width; int height; }
enum CaptionPosition { top, bottom }  enum CaptionSize { small, medium, large }   // CaptionVariant went in M30
enum CaptionLook { box, shadow }  enum BoxColours { whiteOnBlack, blackOnWhite, yellowOnBlack, whiteOnGreen }  enum ShadowColours { white, yellow, blackGlow, gold }   // M37
class CaptionAppearance { CaptionLook look; BoxColours boxColours; ShadowColours shadowColours; }   // M37
class CaptionSettings { String text; CaptionPosition position; CaptionSize size; CaptionAppearance appearance; }
// ReelDraft and FineTuneDraft also carry bool fadeEnds (FR-33, M38) and bool louderSound (FR-42, M52).
class ReelDraft { SourceVideo? video; ScriptLanguage language; Transcript? transcript; LineSelection selection; ClipRange? clip; FrameSettings frame; CaptionSettings caption; }
class RecentReel { String id; String title; String videoPath; String thumbnailPath; Duration duration; OutputSize size; DateTime createdAt; String sourceVideoId; String? youtubeId; List<SourceRange> usedRanges; } // title: first caption line; the source fields since M54 (FR-44), a stored reel without sourceVideoId is unreadable
class SourceRange { Duration start; Duration end; } // M54: in the source video, or the YouTube video for a joined link clip
class JoinedCut { Duration sourceStart; Duration sourceEnd; Duration joinedStart; } // M54: LinkedClip.cuts in link.json, absent before M54
class SavedTranscript { Transcript transcript; String source; DateTime savedAt; } // groq-whisper-large-v3 (captions removed in M23) 
class SavedScript { SourceVideo video; ScriptLanguage language; SavedTranscript saved; } // a Recent row
class FineTuneDraft { TranscriptKey script; RangeSelected selection; ClipRange clip; FrameSettings frame; CaptionSettings caption; bool linesEdited; int scriptFingerprint; DateTime savedAt; } // FR-24; the script itself is not copied
class SavedFineTune { SourceVideo video; FineTuneDraft draft; } // a Recent row (FR-24)
class TopicRequest { LinkedVideo video; ScriptLanguage outputLanguage; } // FR-43, M53: no topic or length; the joined clip takes the video's title
class LinkSession { TopicRequest request; List<ReviewCut>? cuts; ClipTransition transition; bool edited; DateTime savedAt; } // a Recent row (FR-38); cuts null while the script is fetched; JSON version 2, one per linked video in topics/<YouTube ID>/session.json (FR-43, M53)
enum SpeechEngine { onDevice, groq }
class CloudTranscriptionSettings { bool enabled; bool keyChecked; } // the key itself lives only in secure storage
class ClipWindow { Duration start; Duration end; }                  // M23: the whole video (FR-15 removed)
class ClipSource { File file; Duration offset; ClipWindow window; } // M23: the kept file, offset 0
sealed class SceneChoice { FollowFace(track), FollowBody(track), HoldSpot(anchor) } // FR-27, M25; a transient answer (identity equality, holds a FaceTrack), never saved
```

All are immutable, with `copyWith` and value equality.

### 6.2 Time formats

| What | Format | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Clip times | `m:ss.t` | `1:23.0` |
| Line timestamps | `m:ss` | `1:23` |
| Durations | seconds | `18.5 s` |
| Long videos | `h:mm:ss` | `1:02:15` |

### 6.3 Crop maths

The target aspect is `aw:ah` and the source is `sw × sh`. Crop sizes are even integers rounded down, and offsets are whole pixels rounded down. Free crop is computed in the source's own pixels, whatever its size or orientation.
- **Crop to fill:** take the largest `aw:ah` rectangle in the source, vertically centred. Its left edge is 0 (left), (sw − cw)/2 (center) or sw − cw (right). Then scale to the output size.
- **Blur background:** the source scaled to cover the output with `boxblur=20:2`, under the source scaled to the output width and vertically centred.

### 6.3.1 Crop for Media3

`Crop` takes normalised device coordinates: left = 2x/sw − 1, right = 2(x+w)/sw − 1, top = 1 − 2y/sh, bottom = 1 − 2(y+h)/sh. It is followed by `Presentation.createForWidthAndHeight(outW, outH, LAYOUT_SCALE_TO_FIT)`.
- **Blur:**
  - `GaussianBlurWithFrameOverlaid` (σ ≈ 20), then `Presentation`.
  - The effect draws the whole input frame sharp, scaled to fit the output width (or its height for a taller source), over a blurred copy stretched to the output's shape. That blur is stretched, not covering.
  - It can't follow a covering `Presentation`.
  - If it looks wrong on a phone, switch to a two-sequence `Composition`.
- **16:9:** `Presentation` only.

### 6.3.2 Moving crop for Media3 (M19)

With keyframes, `Crop` is replaced by a `MatrixTransformation` whose matrix is computed for each frame from the keyframes. The time is the frame's presentation time counted from the clip start, from one helper (`clipTimeMs`) that the caption overlay uses too, so the crop and the caption lines can't drift apart.
- **Rectangle at a time:** a pure function `ndcRectAt(keyframes, timeMs)` (unit-tested without Android): before the first keyframe the first rectangle; after the last the last; between two a Glide interpolates each edge linearly and a Cut holds the earlier until the later's time, and from that time on is the later. This is the same rule as `CropPath.anchorAt` in Dart.
- **Matrix:** the rectangle in NDC (§6.3.1) scaled to fill the frame (`scale = 2/(right−left)`, `2/(top−bottom)`, translated by `−(right+left)/(right−left)`, `−(top+bottom)/(top−bottom)`), built as a plain 4 × 4 array (no `android.graphics.Matrix`, which unit tests can't use).
- **Size:** `configure` returns the window's pixel size, as `Crop` does, so the `Presentation.createForWidthAndHeight(outW, outH, LAYOUT_STRETCH_TO_FIT)` after it is the same near-1:1 step as the fixed crop's.
- **Unchanged:** the overlay still comes last, and Blur and 16:9 never send keyframes.
- **iOS (M26):** the same rule in Swift (`ios/Runner/Render/MovingCrop.swift`: `ndcRectAt`, `clipTimeMs`). The time is each decoded frame's own presentation time in the clip's composition, which starts at zero, floored to whole milliseconds like Android's `presentationTimeUs / 1000`, so a Cut (the keyframe 10 ms before the cut, FR-27) lands on the same frame as the picture. The window is a Core Image rectangle (`pixelRect`) taken from the whole, already upright frame, scaled to the output and stretched (the same near-1:1 stretch), not a GL matrix, so `glMatrixFor` has no Swift twin. There is no fade at cuts.

### 6.4 Language inference (automatic)

From the transcript text, first match wins:

| Text contains | Language |
|---|---|
| Arabic script with any of `ٹ ڈ ڑ ں ے ھ ہ` | Urdu |
| Arabic script with any of `پ چ ژ گ` | Persian |
| Other Arabic script | Arabic |
| Devanagari | Hindi |
| Bengali script | Bengali |
| Gurmukhi | Punjabi |
| Latin | English |
| Anything else | `und`, shown as "Detected language" |

### 6.5 Caption font by script

| Script | Font |
|---|---|
| Arabic script (Urdu, Arabic, Persian and the like) | Noto Nastaliq Urdu |
| Everything else | Inter; glyphs it lacks fall back to system fonts |

Urdu, Arabic and Persian text is right to left.

### 6.6 Roman Urdu (removed in M30)

Removed with the caption variants (FR-7). It was a rule-based, letter-by-letter transliteration with a short list of common-word overrides.

## 7. Logo

"Trim": trim brackets around a five-bar waveform on a rounded tile, in a 100-unit viewBox. One `LogoPainter` serves the UI, the export overlay and the launcher icon.

| Part | Spec |
|---|---|
| Tile | rx 24 |
| Brackets | `M33 24 H24 V76 H33` and `M67 24 H76 V76 H67`, stroke 6, round caps and joins |
| Bars | x = 38, 44, 50, 56, 62 spanning y 42–58, 35–65, 30–70, 37–63, 43–57, stroke 4.5, round caps |
| Colours | tile `#3DD68C`, ink `#06140D`; dark variant tile `#18201C`, ink `#3DD68C`. The green tile is used in both app themes (FR-32); the logo never follows the theme |

The Android launch screen (FR-20) draws the same shapes as vector drawables (`launch_logo.xml`, `splash_icon.xml`), since it shows before Flutter starts; keep them in step with `LogoPainter`. `LogoPainter` also takes a `LogoPose` (bracket inset, bar half-lengths) for the launch animation; the rest pose is the table above.

## 8. Error states

| Condition | Where | Message | Actions |
|---|---|---|---|
| Invalid link (removed in M23, back in M44; "a YouTube link" until M70) | Source | That doesn't look like a link. | Edit |
| A link from a site yt-dlp can't read (M70, FR-50) | Source (link card) | The downloader can't read videos from this site. | Edit |
| The site wants a login (M70, FR-50) | Source (link card) | This video needs a login to the site, which the app can't do. Try a public video. | Edit |
| Video unavailable, private, age-restricted or live (removed in M23, back in M44) | Source (link card) | This video can't be downloaded. | Edit |
| No network while reading a link (M44) | Source (link card) | You're offline. Connect and try again. | Read link |
| A link over the length cap (M45, FR-36) | Review cuts | This video is too long. Links can be up to 3 hours with captions, or 2 hours without. | Back |
| No moments about the topic (M45, FR-36; removed in M53) | Review cuts | Couldn't find this topic in the video. Try other words or a longer video. | Find again, Back |
| Groq's daily limit used up (M45, FR-36; removed in M53 with the search) | Review cuts | Groq's free daily limit is used up. Try again tomorrow, or use a key on Groq's paid plan. | Back |
| Groq's chat reply isn't the JSON asked for (M45, FR-36) | Review cuts | Groq's answer couldn't be read. Try again. | Find again, Back |
| No MP4 stream to download in parts (M46, FR-37) | Review cuts | This video can't be downloaded in parts. | Back |
| No network while downloading the cuts (M46, FR-37) | Review cuts | You're offline. Connect and try again. Finished cuts are kept. | Try again, Back |
| yt-dlp can't read the streams, or their links keep expiring (M46, FR-37; "from YouTube" until M70) | Review cuts | Couldn't read this video. Update the downloader in Settings, or try again later. Under it, small and selectable, yt-dlp's error (M73). | Try again, Back |
